People at work have started noticing that I'm not keeping up with things, so I figured I needed to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most days, I just wake up with this... heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It honestly just feels like the day is already lost before I've even managed to get out of bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to do a lot of different things—like, I'd plan hikes, take photos, cook big meals on Sundays, and I even volunteered at the library. But now... I don't know, those things just feel like items on a checklist. I just don't care enough to even start them anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about seven months now. It started right around the time my husband stopped working... his company had some downsizing, and he lost his job. Since then, I've just been feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worry or anxiety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I don't feel anxious or worried about things. It's more just... that empty feeling I mentioned. I don't really have the energy to worry, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying time spent with friends and family lately?\n\nPatient: Not really. It's kind of like what I was saying about the other stuff. I used to love having people over or going out, but now... it just feels like another chore. It's like the hikes or the cooking I mentioned before. I just don't have the interest or the energy to engage with anyone right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your physical activity level or movement?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I've just gotten so slowed down. I'm an auditor, and lately... well, I can sit there staring at one audit workpaper for twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type. It's like I just can't get my body to move. It's actually a big part of why I'm here, because people at work have started noticing that I'm not keeping up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I feel completely empty and drained all the time, but I don't want to die. I haven't had any thoughts about hurting myself or anything like that. It's just... Most days, I just wake up with this... heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It's like I just can't get my body to move. It's actually a big part of why I'm here, because people at work have started noticing that I'm not keeping up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's just that same heavy feeling all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt even slightly better for more than a few hours?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, it really doesn't let up at all. I haven't had any stretches of time where I felt even a little bit better. It's just a constant low, day in and day out.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure or enjoyment in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I used to do a lot of things... I'd plan hikes on the weekends, take photos, spend my Sundays cooking meals for the week. I even volunteered at the library. But now... I just don't care. All of those things just feel like items on a checklist that I don't have the energy or the desire to even start. I don't get any enjoyment out of them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety, or have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't been restless at all, it's actually the opposite. It's like I just can't get going. People at work have started noticing that I'm not keeping up with things, so I figured I needed to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most days, I just wake up with this... heavy, hollow feeling in my chest. It honestly just feels like the day is already lost before I've even managed to get out of bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to do a lot of different things—like, I'd plan hikes, take photos, cook big meals on Sundays, and I even volunteered at the library. But now... I don't know, those things just feel like items on a checklist. I just don't care enough to even start them anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been about seven months now. It started right around the time my husband stopped working... his company had some downsizing, and he lost his job. Since then, I've just been feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worry or anxiety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I don't feel anxious or worried about things. It's more just... that empty feeling I mentioned. I don't really have the energy to worry, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying time spent with friends and family lately?\n\nPatient: Not really. It's kind of like what I was saying about the other stuff. I used to love having people over or going out, but now... it just feels like another chore. I just don't have the interest or the energy to engage with anyone right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your physical activity level or movement?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I've just gotten so slowed down. I'm an auditor, and lately... well, I can sit there staring at one audit workpaper for twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type. It's like I just can't get my body to move. It's actually a big part of why I'm here, because people at work have started noticing that I'm not keeping up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I feel completely empty and drained all the time, but I don't want to die. I haven't had any thoughts about hurting myself or anything like that. It's just... I just feel stuck, not suicidal.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely with my appetite. Food has mostly just become something I force down because I know I should eat. I've actually noticed my work skirts have gotten pretty loose lately, even though I'm not trying to lose weight at all. As for sleep, I haven't really noticed as much of a change there, but my appetite is just gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has it been a constant feeling of being down?\n\nPatient: No, there haven't really been any better days. It just stays like that, all day, every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on things like reading or watching TV?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean...
